# Gender Action Plan

## Programme Title: Implementation of the Lao PDR Emission Reductions Program through improved governance and sustainable forest landscape management

This Gender Action Plan (GAP) forms the basis for operationalizing the results and recommendations of the gender analysis. It contains specific gender elements to be considered in all programme activities, including project design, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation components and activities. Moreover, it helps to monitor implementation of these measures and activities. Hence, this document is a key tool for ensuring that the Lao PDR Emission Reductions Program takes into account a gender perspective in the GCF programme in order to maximize climate and development co-benefits.

The GAP ensures that all programme activities are designed with a gender-sensitive approach as well as to manage and mitigate potential adverse impacts over the duration of the programme. The GAP ensures that the programme is compliant with GCF’s gender policy (GCF/01.09).

The GAP is closely aligned with the outputs of the log-frame and planned activities. It complements the project design, implementation, monitoring, evaluation components and activities to consider gender-related aspects. In addition to the specific activities and measures of the GAP, the GCF programme will systematically apply some general measures in accordance with the GIZ Gender Strategy:

- Based on the GIZ’s guidelines on designing and using a results-based monitoring system, the programme will set up adequate gender-sensitive indicators that measure the program's activities have on gender relations by setting up adequate, gender-sensitive results-based monitoring and by collecting sex-disaggregated data.
- A gender-sensitive language is used in reports, training materials and publications.
- In the programme team(s), competencies on gender and gender-sensitive forest management will be considered during the hiring process and further developed through means of training.
- The GAP will be executed by the different entities involved in the programme management, including all concerned Government agencies and development partners.

## Programme overview

Importantly, the project aims to support the Government and people of Laos in changing the present-day use of forests and landscapes and to ensure a transition to sustainable management at scale. This is expected to result in more than 50m tCO2eq over the program's duration.

## Programmatic approach:

The programme design consists of:
- Three Projects:
    - Project 1: Bokeo, Sayaboury and Luang Prabang provinces. Mid-2020 to mid-2024.
    - Project 2: Luang Namtha, Bokeo and Ouham provinces. 2022 to end-2029.
    - Project 3: Xieng Khouang, Phongsaly and Xiangkhoang provinces. Mid-2021 to end-2029.

Each Project contains four Outputs (see below):
> Output 1: Creation of an enabling environment for REDD+


---
**Page 218**
---

- Output 2: Market solutions for agricultural drivers of deforestation
- Output 3: Climate mitigation action through forestry
- Output 4: Strengthening the regulatory framework and reporting

Project 1 and Project 2 share many similarities in terms of their 'on-the-ground' interventions. However, Project 1 also addresses a number of national-level interventions that will also serve to support the implementation of Project 2. A good example for this is the creation of the REDD+ Funding Window which will be undertaken in Project 1 but whose structure and functionality will also be utilized in Project 2.

The approval of a first payment from the GCF for Project 1 will allow the establishment and scale-up of the REDD+ Funding Window. The funding window will finance projects that are performance-based payments to participating communities and institutions to incentivize and fund their participation in the REDD+ programme. The programme lies its performance to MRV-able milestones, providing GIZ and GCF with the necessary information for the approval of Project 1 and Project 3, for which additional funding may be approved subsequently by the GCF Board.

This Funding Proposal seeks approval of GCF funding for Project 1 only. Separate Funding Proposals will be submitted in the future for Project 2 and for Project 3.


| Project 1 | Project 2 | Project 3 |
|---|---|---|
| Xeppa, Sengkam, Luang Prabang (Duration: 8 years) | Phonsavan, Bolaven, Dodomxay (Duration: 8 years) | Sayaboury, Don Det, Luang Prabang (Duration: 10 years) |

Figure 1: GCF Programme to Implement the Lao PDR Emission Reductions Programme

The following specific outputs and activities are planned:

- Output 1: Creation of an enabling environment for REDD+ implementation
    - Activity 1.1: REDD+ Funding Window and sustainable financing
    - Activity 1.2: Strengthening the regulatory framework and socio-economic development plans (SEIDPs)
    - Activity 1.3: Strengthening the regulatory framework
    - Activity 1.4: Law enforcement and monitoring
    - Activity 1.5: Improving land tenure security
    - Activity 1.6: Implementation of the measurement, reporting and verification (MRV) system
    - Activity 1.7: Knowledge management, FPIC, safeguards and gender

- Output 2: Market solutions for agricultural drivers of deforestation
    - Activity 2.1: Local incentives for good agricultural practices (GAP) and agroforestry
    - Activity 2.2: Sustainable land use planning and land-use change
    - Activity 2.3: Sustainable rural infrastructure/watershed management


---
**Page 219**
---

Output 3: Climate mitigation action through forestry
- Activity 3.1: Village forest management
- Activity 3.2: Sub-district management of production forests
- Activity 3.3: National Programme Management

Output 4: Programme management, coordination, monitoring and reporting
- Gender is integrated into all outputs and most activities. The target group survey, which is now considered a baseline study, will be carried out as one field survey towards the end of the 9-5-year programme cycle.

Gender Action Plan

Below the gender action plan for the (i) programme and (ii) Project 1 are presented. As the programme is implemented, additional revisions may be required for the programme-level gender action plan, which will be submitted along with each of the projects.

# Gender Action Plan: Monitoring, evaluation and reporting

A long-term safeguard, gender and M&E specialist as well as a national expert who will support the safeguarding, gender and M&E specialist, will be embedded within the NPMU. They will oversee, guide and monitor the implementation of the gender action plan to ensure the successful implementation of the GAP and ESMP. They will be responsible for monitoring, evaluation and reporting for the GAP.

With a focus on gender, their responsibilities will include (among others):

- Liaising with local partners
- Responsibility for overseeing program communication and stakeholder engagement on topics related to gender
- Revision of program documents/publications/guidelines/policies to ensure effective gender mainstreaming, and that information is gender-sensitive
- Close cooperation with the LWU and LFNC on topics related to gender, building on best practices
- Identification of local and provincial CSOs and women's groups for collaboration on community outreach, information dissemination and other program activities
- Dissemination of information about the grievance mechanism to program partners, local communities and other stakeholders
- Overseeing (implementing, monitoring and reporting) the gender action plan
- Monitoring program progress, including in achieving the ESMP and gender action plan targets, and ensuring adaptive management (as needed).
